<h2><a id="ILLUSTRATIONS"></a>ILLUSTRATIONS</h2>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">The Yosemite Falls, Yosemite National Park</span></td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#Frontispiece" class="pginternal"><i>Frontispiece</i></a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>The total height of the three falls is 2600 feet. The upper fall is
about 1600 feet, and the lower about 400 feet. Mr. Muir was
probably the only man who ever looked down into the heart of the
fall from the narrow ledge of rocks near the top.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Charles S. Olcott</i></span><br></div>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">Sheep in the Mountains</span></td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#photo1" class="pginternal">8</a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>Since the establishment of the Yosemite National Park the pasturing
of sheep has not been allowed within its boundaries, and as a
result the grasses and wild flowers have recovered very much of
their former luxuriance. The flock of sheep here photographed were
feeding near Alger Lake on the slope of Blacktop Mountain, at an
altitude of about 10,000 feet and just beyond the eastern boundary
of the Park.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Herbert W. Gleason</i></span><br></div>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">A Silver Fir, or Red Fir</span> (<i>Abies magnifica</i>)</td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#photo2" class="pginternal">90</a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>This tree was found in an extensive forest of red fir above the
Middle Fork of King’s River. It was estimated to be about 250 feet
high. Mr. Muir, on being shown the photograph, remarked that it was
one of the finest and most mature specimens of the red fir that he
had ever seen.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Herbert W. Gleason</i></span><br></div>
<table style="border-spacing: 2px;padding: 2px;border-width: 0px;width: 100%;" class="xhtml_table_width" data-summary="CONTENTS">
<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: left;"><span class="smcap">The North and South Domes</span></td>
<td style="text-align: right;"><a href="#photo3" class="pginternal">122</a></td></tr>
</tbody></table>
<div class="blockquot"><p>The great rock on the right is the South Dome, commonly called the
Half-Dome, according to Mr. Muir “the most beautiful and most
sublime of all the Yosemite rocks.” The one on the left is the
North Dome, while in the center is the Washington Column.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Charles S. Olcott</i></span><br></div>

the Peak, on a day when the “cloud mountains” so inspiring to Mr.
Muir were much in evidence.</p>
<span style="margin-left: 2em;"><i>From a photograph by Herbert W. Gleason</i></span><br></div>

The manual marks illustrations as <figure> with <figcaption> and a z3998:photograph or illustration epub:type, not as <p class="figcenter"> and <p class="caption">. se-llm also wrongly wraps the caption text in <i epub:type="se:name.visual-art.illustration">, which is for naming a work of art and not for a caption of the picture itself.

se-llm step: Name and order sections
SE correct stray hr / section structure
The Gutenberg hr at the end of a chapter is a visual divider, not a scene break. SE closes the section cleanly with no hr, while se-llm leaves it inside the section.

se-llm correct body z3998 type
The book is a non-fiction memoir, so the SE rule for z3998:non-fiction applies. SE's z3998:fiction on these files is a mis-tag. Gutenberg gives no basis for fiction.
markup only in SE <hgroup> · only in se-llm nothing
se-llm step: se-llm has no markup here
SE correct heading markup
SE's heading rules wrap a chapter's number and title in an hgroup, with the title in a p epub:type="title". se-llm leaves the Gutenberg-style separate headings with no hgroup.

se-llm step: Name and order sections

se-llm step: Name and order sections
SE correct stray hr / section structure
The Gutenberg hr at the end of a chapter is a visual divider, not a scene break. SE closes the section cleanly with no hr, while se-llm leaves it inside the section.
markup only in SE <body epub:type="bodymatter z3998:fiction"> · only in se-llm <body epub:type="bodymatter z3998:non-fiction">

se-llm correct body z3998 type
The book is a non-fiction memoir, so the SE rule for z3998:non-fiction applies. SE's z3998:fiction on these files is a mis-tag. Gutenberg gives no basis for fiction.
markup only in SE <hgroup> · only in se-llm nothing
se-llm step: se-llm has no markup here
SE correct heading markup
SE's heading rules wrap a chapter's number and title in an hgroup, with the title in a p epub:type="title". se-llm leaves the Gutenberg-style separate headings with no hgroup.

The book is a non-fiction memoir, so the SE rule for z3998:non-fiction applies. SE's z3998:fiction on these files is a mis-tag. Gutenberg gives no basis for fiction.

se-llm step: se-llm has no markup here
SE correct heading markup
SE's heading rules wrap a chapter's number and title in an hgroup, with the title in a p epub:type="title". se-llm leaves the Gutenberg-style separate headings with no hgroup.

both acceptable time element
The manual permits time elements around dates, and SE simply does not tag these datelines. The date is still left in Gutenberg's italic run-in form, which is the real flaw and is covered by the dateline patterns.

markup only in SE </blockquote> · only in se-llm nothing
se-llm step: se-llm has no markup here
SE correct structure / blockquote
SE wraps the diary-style body in blockquote z3998:diary-entry elements, so its structure ends with a closing blockquote. se-llm has no such wrapper because it did not apply diary-entry markup. SE's structure follows the manual.

se-llm step: kept from the Gutenberg draft
SE edition difference dropped/added material
SE left out the book's index and put a map caption in its place. se-llm kept the Gutenberg index text.

SE edition difference section structure
The extra closing section comes from SE's different book structure: SE nests the map and text in an additional section and drops the Gutenberg index. This is not a tagging error by either edition, so it comes from SE's own structure and source choices.
